# Body modeler log

## Profile hosts in the construction tree: follow-up for the universal-algorithm PR (f1_integration2), recorded 2026-11-08 17:38 +09:00

S00 stays a loft chain (main's decision). A profile leaf (`AutoMovieConstructionProfileRealize` -> `AutoMovieConstructionProfileBody.append`) publishes faces with `meshLoops` and no `state.chains` entry. None of the three operators that attach to S00 admits such a host today:
- Continuation: `packages/engine/src/geometry/construction/AutoMovieConstructionContinuationRealize.ts:37` requires the host chain. `AutoMovieConstructionContinuation.apply` copies its end layer's coefficients into the part chain, and `AutoMovieConstructionRing` takes `hostChain.samples`. A profile end has no layer to copy, so a profile-end to loft-start boundary is a new operator. Without it the head cannot attach.
- Known port: `AutoMovieConstructionPortRealize.ts:42` refuses on `!state.chains.has(hostFace)`, although the port reads only the host surface's jets on its chart (station mm, phase rad). `AutoMovieSolidBody.draw` meshes a hole cycle together with a face's `meshLoops`. The change: admit a chainless face when the port loop lies inside one piece's chart, inside its phase range when the piece is not periodic.
- Split: `AutoMovieConstructionSplitRealize.ts:43` requires the host chain for the end station, the pole check and the end layer's `frame` (the outward normal). It reads both arcs as phases `[a, a + 2 pi]` of one periodic face. A profile end is a `meshLoops` start/end that the split's end cycle would duplicate. The change: the station from the face domain, the normal from the profile end's plane, end ownership through `meshLoops`, and one periodic piece per end loop. A multi-piece (figure-8) end needs its arcs across pieces. A tilted planar end is already admitted for a chain host: the normal comes from the end layer's own `frame`.

## Arm forms, contact, dome and shoulder join (f1_arm), recorded 2026-11-08 09:40 +09:00

- Upper-arm forms from published tables (`AutoMovieHumanUpperArmForm`): Sanada 2006 muscle and Maruyama 1991 fat split the biceps-site depth about the humerus axis (women -0.16 mm, practically centred); Akagi 2011 flexor thickening less Maruyama anterior fat gives the anterior slope at the biceps site (+0.033); Larsen 2025 deltoid thinning plus Maruyama lateral fat the lateral slope at the axilla (-0.085); the deltoid insertion at 49 % from GH to the elbow (Huri 2014, Morgan 2006). Debts: the triceps belly station (no baseline posterior profile in Wakahara 2012/2013, Sugisaki 2010/2015, Maeo 2022, Matta 2011) and the thenar (no thenar law without an adducted thumb in White 1980, Greiner 1991, the APB and adductor pollicis ultrasound literature).
- Right arm and digits served by the same provider through their own ISB frames.
- Chest-wall contact (`AutoMovieHumanArmContact`): the abduction at GH solved at build time so the least signed gap of the skin below the axilla to S00 is zero; 3.25 deg on the current trunk, -27 mm unabducted. Medial-half samples and a tolerance-bounded bisection took it from 18.6 to 9.1 s.
- Dome: the whole walk in the engine's two-pole angle parameter, the pole on the humerus axis at the acromion level (ANSUR's acromion is a skin landmark; a skin-over-acromion proxy double-counted and was removed). OOM cause: binary64 sin(pi) in the palm pole's chain rule; ds/dx is exactly zero at both poles.
- Shoulder join through the body tree (`AutoMovieHumanArmShoulderJoin`): fold radius 6.82 mm (Maruyama medial upper arm J14 and mid-axillary C5 fat), cap 49.6 mm (half the acromion-axilla height). Traces were lost while the dome pole grazed S00's edge (the footprint pinched about the pole; the port centre lay outside it, angle span -2.32..0.29 rad); the cause was S00 missing its own acromion by about 9 mm, fixed by f1_trunk. With S00 through the acromion the phase trace realizes: trunk, arm and blend faces, one skin. Open: a 9 mm notch at the top, pleats along the seam, and the host lacking S00's reliefs (the construction tree realizes the bare view part; `IAutoMovieViewPart.reliefs` in progress).

## Trunk S00 (f1_trunk), recorded 2026-11-08 06:14 +09:00

Method: S00 is one level-section stream from the open crotch end to the level neck-base end at the cervicale height, drawn by fair C2 outlines in the station (anterior, posterior, lateral, shoulder, front-quarter departures) with the breasts, buttocks and thigh-root fronts as engine reliefs; girths are closed site by site.

- Frames: the thorax stands on the pelvis by the measured chain (supervisor approval 05:18), replacing Kendall's acromion-over-hip ideal. The turn's half length is set by the concave-side extent only.

- Rounds (f1): heel as a 103 mm ball (heel extent solved from the tape at null fullness against a 16.8 mm anterior) -> anterior on the ankle tape line, heel by the bisection ray to the pternion line: overshoot 7.3 mm, floor dip 6.9 mm -> heel hull datums: 4.3 / 3.1 -> profile curve, 3 datums per half-turn: 1.2 / 4.8 -> value-and-slope datums: 1.4 / 0.32 -> actual extents at the ankle knot: 0.82 / 3.9 with the heel-ankle ring 0.22 mm short, caused by the loft reading the stretched extent for the turn length (fixed; re-render pending). ### Gluteal parent and construction-order alternatives, source0cd276

BodyLane currently joins every trunk mass before the head, legs and arms. TrunkBuild gives each mass the trunk seed face. BodyTree.join nevertheless passes the whole host subtree to the engine, and realizeAutoMovieConstruction builds a captured atlas of its faces; the trace can cross a retained neighbour boundary. The seed search starts on its one named host face. Adding legs to the earlier subtree can therefore change the available host without changing a mass view, but neither the order alone nor overlapping projected outlines proves successful seeding or volume containment.

The MassPart embedded start is a full regular footprint, not a closed root pole. Its four local circular sagittae plus amplitude/10 are an embedding estimate rather than a global host bound. The existing `inside the layers` diagnostic checks a traced intersection's part/host station domains. It does not itself prove that a root point lies outside a closed host volume. The gluteal bounded-seed failure likewise establishes no exhaustive absence of intersection.

| Alternative | Exterior preservation and actual prerequisite |
| --- | --- |
| Keep the present source and order | Preserve every original value and jet, and distinguish a bounded seed/trace failure from a source defect. If a valid owned intersection exists, the missing kernel behavior belongs to the engine rather than a source fit. |

The pole argument uses the actual source parameter, independently of its implementation: with epsilon = pi - theta and H the whole pole-station span, `top - s = H sin^2(epsilon/2) = H epsilon^2/4 + O(epsilon^4)`. A negative nonzero top theta tangent and zero second theta derivative give `r = m epsilon + O(epsilon^3)`, m positive, hence a positive radius and a quadratic local height-radius relation. The physical derivative dr/ds diverges there; a finite normalized-angle jet is therefore not a finite station jet. This is the source meridian condition. Angular section behavior and final coefficient/phase interpolation still prevent claiming complete surface G2 from it. The shared signed control hull, rather than sampled pole-adjacent values, owns the scalar span admission.

I directly opened the primary [Buryanov 2010 article](https://www.scielo.cl/scielo.php?lng=en&pid=S0717-95022010000300015&script=sci_arttext), [Kanehisa 2013 article](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1186/1880-6805-32-4), Reddy's primary table text and [MIT's Bezier definition and convex-hull property](https://web.mit.edu/hyperbook/Patrikalakis-Maekawa-Cho/node12.html). The modeling responsibility is concrete:

- The axis owns each station's world origin and section frame. The front and side positive/negative outlines own the four signed chart extremes. On the trunk the engine's front pair is the anatomical side profile and its side pair the left/right breadth. A source curve in the wrong frame is a placement defect even if its scalar breadth is correct.
- Top/fullness owns the four quadrants between those extremes. A girth solves a conic shoulder inside the declared box; a sourced top shape supplies the shoulders. `ViewLoft` evaluates the natural outline splines, with its pole sigma mapping, makes `ViewSection` conic/polar-quintic sections, interpolates periodic B-spline coefficients and publishes C2 station jets. A final skin defect must not be hidden by distorting an otherwise correct defining curve.
- The leg's whole-skin front split cannot be justified by Kanehisa's one-site muscle thickness. Separately, knot-only median clamps do not constrain a whole natural-cubic span. Core has the requested general C2/half-space curve contract; body must provide the correct source definition and full-span counterexample.
- Foot breadth is an independent metatarsal-head measurement but `FootInterim` currently multiplies every transverse value by length. Fixing that omission requires the same ball cut, axis and head record for the source outlines and joints; a first-MTP-only transverse cut cannot silently become an oblique ball width.
- Palm length reaches a skin crease; Buryanov's external finger length begins at a web, and his web-to-MCP height is another length. The new `HandBones` must stop using `handLength - palm` as a measured web-to-tip length. A separately named crease station must consume the supplied palm length instead of letting that input drift into a different datum.
- Whole RGB images hide occlusion, reference support gaps and cropped ends. Site measurements cannot reveal between-site crossing, and two outline pairs alone do not establish top fullness. Each complete signed outline, its top sections and axis, and its neighbors must be compared against the same reference from orthogonal and useful oblique directions.

Phase 0, design only: no construction code. Read before writing: #3044, #3047 to #3051, #3045, #3046, `modeler-log.md`'s hand-off, next-campaign and merge-and-diff sections, the human and 3d-modeling skills, `surface-composition.md`, `subtractive-pieces.md`, `seam-registry.md`, `section-chain-lofting.md`, `generalization.md` (trunk, leg, foot, arm and hand), the trunk, leg, arm, foot and hand sources, and the kernel's interfaces committed in `8194f8923` (`IAutoMovieHumanViewPart`, `AutoMovieHumanViewAxis`, `AutoMovieHumanViewSection`, `AutoMovieHumanViewLoft`, `AutoMovieHumanSolidChain.framed`, `IJoin.continuity`).

Paths I will own in phase 1 (named now; no file touched in phase 0 but this section): the trunk's files under `packages/human/src/generation/` (`AutoMovieHumanTrunk*`, `IAutoMovieHumanTrunk*`, `AutoMovieHumanBreast*`, `AutoMovieHumanGluteal*`, `AutoMovieHumanSpinal*`, `AutoMovieHumanUmbilic*`, `AutoMovieHumanSternalNotch*`, `AutoMovieHumanScapularMass`, `AutoMovieHumanShoulder*`, `AutoMovieHumanBodyGirth`, `AutoMovieHumanLimbRootStrip`, `AutoMovieHumanLimbFrame`), `generation/leg/`, `generation/arm/`, `generation/foot/`, `generation/hand/`, `generation/web/`, `AutoMovieHumanLegConstruction`, `AutoMovieHumanArmConstruction`, `AutoMovieHumanFootConstruction`, `AutoMovieHumanBodyWeb`, `AutoMovieHumanSkeletonLeg`, `AutoMovieHumanSkeletonShoulder`, and their tests. Shared with other lanes, to be settled with the supervisor before phase 1 (below): `AutoMovieHumanBodyLaw` (the order of the head, neck and trunk and its `EVENTS`), `AutoMovieHumanNeckConstruction` and `AutoMovieHumanTrunkNeckSeat` (who owns the neck base), `AutoMovieHumanEventJoin*` (also joins the head's chains), and the tessellation of joins (`AutoMovieHumanBodySurface*`, `AutoMovieHumanBodyRoots`, `AutoMovieHumanBodyStrip`).

### Rules every body part follows

1. Each part is one `IAutoMovieHumanViewPart` lofted by `AutoMovieHumanViewLoft.create`, under its existing chain name where the chain survives (`trunk`, `leg.<side>`, `arm.<side>`, `foot.<side>`, `toe.<side>.<n>`, `hand.<side>.1`, `finger.<side>.<name>`, `thumb.<side>`). The left side is canonical and the right is its reflection (J6).
2. Naming map. The kernel names a part's views by its chart axes: `front` holds the extremes along `u`, `side` the extremes along `v`. Every body part takes `u` forward (anterior, or dorsal on the hand and foot) and `v` lateral (+Z on the left side), so the kernel's `front` pair is the anatomical side view (front and back profiles) and its `side` pair is the anatomical front view (lateral and medial outlines). Every table below names the anatomical view.
3. Knots stand at published sites, and each knot's value is an input read there, or a law with a published source or a derivation. Where no breadth or depth is published at a tape site, the box comes from the girth and a width-to-depth law plus a law placing the bone or the axis inside the box. Those laws are the input and laws worker's (owner rule 3 lists the priors they replace); each one this map needs is listed as "law needed" with its candidate source, and none is fitted on f1, f2, m1 or m2.
4. Fullness. Where tapes exist the part's fullness is pre-solved at each tape site by `AutoMovieHumanViewSection.solve` on that station's box, and the part is handed to the operator as `kind: "shoulder"` with those solved shoulders as knots plus a sourced shoulder law at the stations between and beyond the tapes. This keeps one owner kind per part while a trunk or limb has both tapes and untaped stretches. Quarter departures only where a source gives them.
5. Joins by role. The merge traces its curve as a graph over the part's phase (`AutoMovieHumanSolidIntersection` premise), so the part is the solid whose station lines leave the host across the contact: the limb onto the trunk, the leg (shank end) onto the foot, the neck onto the trunk, each digit onto the palm's or forefoot's distal cap, a breast or a gluteal mass along its own outward axis, a pit cutter along its inward axis. The union is symmetric; only the operator's roles follow this rule.
6. Joints inside one bone chain are walk stations, not merges: the knee, the elbow, the wrist, and the interphalangeal joints of fingers and toes. At the neutral pose those segments are within a few degrees of collinear, so the two tubes' surfaces cross nearly tangentially, where the trace's Jacobian `[A_s, -B_y, -B_theta]` is singular; #3048 and #3050 already allow this reduction for the knee and the elbow ("the C2 station rule being its blend"). The part's axis is then two or more straight bone segments joined by a C2 turn over the joint's skin depth (`IAutoMovieHumanViewAxis.IAxis` is an interface, so the jointed axis is the limb's own piece, carrying today's eased-turn law of `AutoMovieHumanLegAxis`). This departs from #3049 and #3051 (phalanx onto phalanx, palm onto forearm); the supervisor is told.
7. Blend widths from the parts' own sizes. A fold between two solids whose outward normals turn by `beta` across the curve (read from both surfaces' jets at each traced point) is the fillet of radius `R` whose tangent points stand `R tan(beta / 2)` from the curve on each side (two faces turning by `beta` touch a circle of radius `R` at that distance from their edge, a derivation). `R` is the soft tissue that rolls into the fold: the subcutaneous layer at the site (`IAutoMovieHumanSubcutaneousFat`, uncompressed layer thickness: `thighFront` at the groin, `thighBack` at the gluteal fold, `upperArmBack` and `chest` at the axilla, `chest` under a breast, `abdomen` at the navel's rim), so a lean person's folds are crisp and shallow and a high-fat person's wide, as `testing.md` requires; for a web between digits `R` is the smaller digit's half breadth at the contact, since the web spans the gap between the two digits' surfaces. Each width is capped at half the gap to a neighbouring merge's host rail (crotch, cleavage, gluteal cleft, webs; `AutoMovieHumanSolidMerge` JSDoc) and at the part's own length to its first section clear of the host.
8. Every part runs into its host far enough that every phase's station line crosses the host inside the part's own layers (`AutoMovieHumanLoftJet.at` clamps outside them): a limb continues to its proximal joint centre and closes there by a pole inside the host; a mass or pit starts inside its host.

### Trunk (#3047)

| Item | Design |
| --- | --- |
| Chain | one chain `trunk` from the perineum (bottom pole, at the crotch height of `AutoMovieHumanLegCrotchHeight`, ISO 8559-1 5.7.6 inseam plus malleolus) to a top pole inside the neck above the neck base. `upper` is retired: the arm no longer lives in the trunk's ring (the hand-off's "the host must be one chain"). Consumers of `upper` to move in the same batch: `AutoMovieHumanSkinChart`, `AutoMovieHumanSkinMarks`, `AutoMovieHumanNeckSurface`, `arm/AutoMovieHumanArmShoulder`, `AutoMovieHumanSkeletonShoulder`, `AutoMovieHumanArmConstruction`, `AutoMovieHumanBodyLaw` `EVENTS`. |
| Frame | level planes at world height (`u` +X, `v` +Z), so every tape and breadth is read horizontally as ISO 8559-1 and ANSUR II define them. The polar section needs its axis strictly inside each box, and the body frame's `x = 0` is not guaranteed inside every person's lower trunk under the posture inputs, so the axis is a level frame whose origin runs on the mid-depth curve `c(y) = (front(y) + back(y)) / 2` of the side profiles (a C2 curve, axes fixed): an `IAxis` of the trunk's own; `AutoMovieHumanViewAxis.horizontal` is its special case `c = 0`. |
| Stations | the published sites as kept rows (`AutoMovieHumanTrunkRows.stations`, at most 3.5 mm apart): crotch (pole), gluteal furrow (`glutealFurrowHeight`), buttock (hip girth, buttock depth), hip breadth's level, high hip, iliocristale, omphalion, natural waist (`waistHeight`), underbust (fold), chest (chest girth, breadth, depth), axilla (`axillaHeight`), acromion, side neck point, suprasternale, cervicale, top pole. |
| Front view (lateral outline, half breadth) | chest `chestBreadth / 2` (ANSUR II: across the rib cage under the arms, tissue compressed: the torso without the arm, which is what this part is), waist `waistBreadth / 2`, hip `hipBreadth / 2` at its level (#3045 item 3), acromion `biacromialBreadth / 2` plus the soft tissue over the acromion, side neck point from the neck base girth (law needed: the trapezius line's height and slope, candidate `faa-1965-shoulder-slope.md`), the top pole on the axis. Underbust, high hip and iliocristale have girths and no breadth: their half breadths come from the girth with the width-to-depth law (law needed; `generalization.md` cites widths following the tapes at about 0.8 over 67 people, source to be named). Below the hip's level the outline closes to the perineum pole: the pelvic floor's envelope, with no published shape, drawn as the least-information conic (the ellipse's shoulder) from the hip level's vertical tangent to the pole's horizontal one; it is visible only between the thighs, and the leg merge decides where it shows. `bicristalBreadth` is bony and places the iliac crests in the skeleton, not a skin knot (#3045). |
| Side view (front and back profiles) | the back profile from the spine's posture (`IAutoMovieHumanPosture`: sacral slope, lumbar lordosis, thoracic kyphosis, cervicale at `cervicaleHeight`), as `AutoMovieHumanSpinalProfile` places it today; the front profile is the back plus the depth at each knot: `chestDepth` less the breast's amplitude at the chest, `waistDepth` at the natural waist, `buttockDepth` less the gluteal amplitude at the buttock. Suprasternale and cervicale are knots of the front and back profile at their published heights, so the neck merge's curve passes through both (below). |
| Fullness | pre-solved shoulders at the tape sites (rule 4). At the waist, high hip and underbust the tape wraps the base alone. At the chest and the buttock the tape wraps the merged skin, so the shoulder there is solved on the length of the convex hull of the station's section curves of the base and the masses merged at that height (a 2D hull of curves, no mesh), which needs the breasts and gluteal masses built before the base's fullness (their frames read only the outlines, not the fullness, so the order closes). Front and back quarters take departures only from a source (law needed: the abdomen's and the back's fullness, candidate the 67-person data `generalization.md` cites). |
| Representation | 256 controls (`order` 256), four guides at the polar angles 0, pi/2, pi, 3 pi/2: front midline, left lateral, back midline, right lateral. Today's seven guides with fixed lateral shares (`AutoMovieHumanTrunkGuides`: bust line 0.65, axillary lines 0.9, scapular line 0.55) become observations. Whether one conic per quarter carries the trunk's front (flat sternum between rounded flanks) and back (flat scapular plate) is the measured risk: judged by the identity test and the views of the first build; if it fails, the quarters' departures need a sourced law, not more guides. |
| Shoulder girdle | the trunk part above the axilla is the torso without the arm: the clavicle, the scapular plate and the trapezius, the lateral outline rising from the chest wall to the acromion and turning down the trapezius line to the side neck point. The deltoid is the arm's. |
| Neck base | the trunk is closed above the neck base: above the side neck points the lateral outline turns in to the top pole on the axis, inside the neck. The head modeler's neck part runs down into the trunk; the neck base ring is the merge's curve, seeded at cervicale and suprasternale, which both parts carry as knots (both surfaces hold those two points, so the curve passes through them). What the trunk publishes for the head modeler: its surface (`AutoMovieHumanViewLoft` `surface`), its neck base landmarks (cervicale, suprasternale, both side neck points) and the blend's fat layer. This reverses today's order, where the neck chain is built first and the trunk copies its bottom layer (`AutoMovieHumanTrunkNeckSeat`, `AutoMovieHumanBodyLaw.calibrate`). |
| Breast (each side) | its own part: straight axis along the base's outward normal at the bust point (height the chest point, lateral place `bustPointWidth / 2`), stations from inside the base to the apex (pole). Front view of the part: its footprint, upper pole at the second rib, lower pole at the inframammary fold (two intercostal spaces under the nipple's fourth rib, Gray 1918, today's `FOLD_DROP`), medial edge at the sternal edge, lateral at the anterior axillary line; side view: the projection profile, amplitude the chest depth less the underbust depth (#3047). Underbust depth is not published, so it needs a law (law needed: the chest wall's depth at the underbust from the underbust girth and the chest breadth). Lower pole fuller than the upper: a quarter departure with a source (candidate: Mallucci and Branford 2012, J Plast Reconstr Aesthet Surg 65:8-16, upper to lower pole 45:55, a lead to verify). Merge onto the base; the inframammary fold is that merge's blend; the cleavage is where the two breasts' blends meet (width cap; touching breasts are a third contact, below). Built for every person; an amplitude under the blend radius leaves the part out without a diagnostic, since a flat chest keeps its contract. |
| Gluteal mass (each side) | its own part along the base's backward normal at the buttock point: footprint from the iliac crest (iliocristale), the sacrum's edge (medial), the gluteal furrow (`glutealFurrowHeight`, inferior) and the greater trochanter's back (lateral; law needed); amplitude the buttock depth less the waist depth (#3047). The gluteal cleft is the blend where the two masses meet at the midline, and the gluteal fold is the blend between the mass and the thigh (#3048): both need a host that is already a merge (gap 1 below). |
| Navel | a diff: a pit cutter along the abdominal wall's inward normal at omphalion (ANSUR II height share, midline), its rim and depth from the `abdomen` fat layer and a published navel size (law needed: today's `TrunkPrior.navel` 11 by 6 mm is unsourced). |
| Spinal groove | a diff along the back midline from the sacral level to the thoracic level, depth from the `back` fat layer and the erector columns (law needed). The groove lies along its host, which the trace cannot follow (gap 2 below); until the kernel answers, it stays out, never a carve along rays (#3044 acceptance 4). |
| Replaces | `AutoMovieHumanTrunkGuides`, `AutoMovieHumanTrunkRows`' role as row table (its spacing rule stays as the station spacing), the section rows of `AutoMovieHumanTrunkPrior`, `AutoMovieHumanTrunkShapes`, `AutoMovieHumanTrunkKeys`, `AutoMovieHumanTrunkColumn`, `AutoMovieHumanTrunkQuadrant`, `AutoMovieHumanTrunkAssembly`, `AutoMovieHumanTrunkSolver`/`Solve`/`Closure`/`Tapes` (the tape and course closure becomes the pre-solve of rule 4), `AutoMovieHumanBodyGirth`'s girth scaling, `AutoMovieHumanTrunkReliefNet`, `AutoMovieHumanBreastRelief`, `AutoMovieHumanGlutealRelief`, `AutoMovieHumanTrunkGlutealCleft`, `AutoMovieHumanTrunkGroinWidth`, `AutoMovieHumanTrunkInguinalFold`, `AutoMovieHumanTrunkCrotchRing`, `AutoMovieHumanTrunkRing`, `AutoMovieHumanShoulderRing`, the `upper` chain and the trunk-down and upper-down events. Kept where sourced: `AutoMovieHumanTrunkLandmarks` (ANSUR II), `AutoMovieHumanSpinalProfile`, the course readings as identity checks. Each feature file's laws are read again at the build and kept only with a source. |

### Leg (#3048)

| Item | Design |
| --- | --- |
| Chain | `leg.<side>`, one walk from a pole inside the foot below the ankle centre, up the tibia, through the knee by a C2 turn, up the femur past the gluteal furrow to a pole inside the pelvis beyond the hip centre (rules 6 and 8). The thigh and the shank are the walk's two straight segments; the knee is a station. |
| Frame | jointed axis on the bind skeleton's ankle, knee and hip centres (`AutoMovieHumanSkeletonLeg`: Hara 2016 hip, the knee on the hip-to-ankle line, the ankle at the lateral malleolus height), `u` anterior, `v` lateral. ISO 8559-1 takes the leg's girths horizontal; the leg stands within a few degrees of vertical, so the square section and the horizontal tape differ by the cosine of that tilt, read back by the identity test. The knee's neutral angle is measured on f1, f2, m1, m2 and the population ends at the build (#3048) and decides only the turn's size. |
| Stations and tapes | ankle (`ankleCircumference`, ISO 8559-1 5.3.26), calf (`calfCircumference` at the calf's widest, 5.3.24), knee (`kneeCircumference` at mid-patella, 5.3.22), lower thigh (`lowerThighCircumference`), thigh (`thighCircumference` at the widest girth just below the gluteal fold, 5.3.20, placed by `glutealFurrowHeight`). The calf's site is where the girth peaks, so its station needs a law (law needed; today's 0.335 to 0.345 of the shank is read on the references and does not stand). |
| Front view (lateral and medial outlines) | at the knee the condyles: `kneeBreadth` (bony) plus the soft tissue over each epicondyle; at the ankle `bimalleolarBreadth` (bony) plus soft tissue, the lateral malleolus lower and further back than the medial (Gray 1918), so each side outline peaks at its own malleolus's height; elsewhere from the girth and the width-to-depth law, split about the bone by a law placing the femur and tibia in the section (law needed; candidate: a cross-section atlas such as Eycleshymer and Schoemaker 1911, A cross-section anatomy, a lead to verify). The calf's medial outline peaks lower than its lateral, the gastrocnemius' medial head descending further (Gray 1918; OBS-LG-1), with the station offset needing a quantitative source (law needed). Above the thigh's site the outlines close toward the hip pole inside the pelvis: construction, never visible, held inside the trunk at every station (checked, rule 8). |
| Side view (anterior and posterior profiles) | from the girth and the width-to-depth law, the patella's front at mid-patella (below), the hamstrings and the calf's back from the same law; the Achilles tendon's narrow back above the heel is a knot of the posterior profile at the ankle. |
| Fullness | pre-solved at the five tape sites, shoulder law between (rule 4). |
| Inside | patella: a merge, a small part along the anterior normal at mid-patella, size from a published patellar dimension (law needed); popliteal hollow: a diff, a pit along the posterior normal at the knee centre between the hamstring tendons, depth bounded by `thighBack` and `calf`; the hollows beside the Achilles tendon: two pits (diffs) behind the malleoli. Vastus medialis and the gastrocnemius heads lie along the leg: they wait on gap 2 and are carried by the outlines' knots meanwhile. |
| Join to the trunk | the leg is the part, the trunk the host (rule 5). Seed at the inguinal fold's front point (on the inguinal ligament line from the anterior superior iliac spine to the pubic tubercle, skeleton landmarks), the curve passing the gluteal furrow behind and the perineum's side medially. The groin fold and the gluteal fold are its blend, `R` from `thighFront` and `thighBack`. At the lateral hip the two surfaces cross at a small angle (the hip's breadth outline closing toward the perineum against the thigh's lateral outline); the angle is read at every traced point against the trace's grazing bound and reported, never stepped over. |
| Replaces | `AutoMovieHumanLegSectionRows`, the row channels of `AutoMovieHumanLegGuides` and `AutoMovieHumanLegProfile`, `AutoMovieHumanLegSectionLaw`'s girth scaling, `AutoMovieHumanLegGirth`, `AutoMovieHumanLegMassPrior`, `AutoMovieHumanLegCrotchRoot`, `AutoMovieHumanLegCrotchFillet`, `AutoMovieHumanLegAnkleSeam`, `AutoMovieHumanLegReliefs` and `AutoMovieHumanLegReliefWindows` (ray reliefs), the leg's use of `AutoMovieHumanLimbRootStrip`, and every crotch-ring reader the hand-off lists (`LegSectionLaw` top end, `LegGirth` top knot, `LegCentreLine` crotch end, `LegAxis` crotch reach, `LegChain` hip depth). Kept: `AutoMovieHumanLegCrotchHeight`, `AutoMovieHumanLegBones`, `AutoMovieHumanLegStations`' spacing rule. |

### Foot (#3049)

| Item | Design |
| --- | --- |
| Chain | `foot.<side>`, a straight axis along the foot's length (pternion to the second toe's line, ANSUR II foot length's axis) on the floor, `u` up (dorsal), `v` lateral; stations from a pole at the heel to a pole beyond the metatarsal heads, so the forefoot ends in a distal cap the toes leave across. |
| Views | top (the sole, the kernel's `kind: "top"`): the sole's outline from `footLength`, `footBreadth` at the metatarsal heads (the first at `ballOfFootLength`, the others by Maestro's cascade as today), `heelBreadth`; the medial arch as the top view's departure of the medial-plantar quarter. Side: dorsum from the ankle (`ankleHeight`, the lateral malleolus) and `navicularHeight` to the forefoot; plantar edge on the floor (the sole stands on the floor by construction, the lower outline being `y = 0` where the foot bears). |
| Join | the leg's shank end is the part merged onto the foot (rule 5: the shank's vertical station lines cross the dorsum, the heel's top and both malleolar sides once at every phase; the foot's horizontal station lines pass under the shank's pole and do not cross it). The heel's back stays below the Achilles tendon's insertion so the shank meets it transversally. |
| Malleoli | the shank's side outlines carry them (leg, above). As merged masses they would sit on the shank-foot blend, a host that is already a merge (gap 1). |
| Toes | each toe its own walk on its line from its metatarsophalangeal centre (inside the forefoot) to its tip (pole), its interphalangeal joints as turns (rule 6), lengths and breadths from `AutoMovieHumanFootToeSpan`, `AutoMovieHumanFootToeBreadth`, `AutoMovieHumanFootToeSpacing` (each re-read for its source at the build). Each merged onto the forefoot's distal cap; the webs are the blends, `R` the smaller toe's half breadth, capped at half the gap between neighbouring host rails, the web saddles closed as corners (gap 3). Neighbouring toes that touch are separate solids in contact, not merged: a merge of two tangent tubes is the trace's singular case. |
| Nails | kept as today's plate on the digit chain's own surface (`0823bce71`, not a ray or mesh read); a merged plate and cut folds lie along their host (gap 2). |
| Replaces | `AutoMovieHumanFootFan`, `AutoMovieHumanFootWebRing`, `AutoMovieHumanFootWebLine`, `AutoMovieHumanFootToeRoot`, `AutoMovieHumanFootToeRootFlare`, `AutoMovieHumanFootEdgeHandover`, `AutoMovieHumanFootGuideSmoothing`, `AutoMovieHumanFootMalleolusBump`, `AutoMovieHumanFootMalleolusWindow`, `AutoMovieHumanFootPrior` (plane cuts of the references), the guide and sector files the views replace, and the foot's use of `generation/web`. |

### Arm (#3050)

| Item | Design |
| --- | --- |
| Chain | `arm.<side>`, one walk from a pole inside the girdle beyond the glenohumeral centre, down the humerus, through the elbow by a C2 turn (the carrying angle, measured on the people at the build), down the forearm, through the wrist into the palm (below), rules 6 and 8. |
| Frame | jointed axis on `AutoMovieHumanArmBones`' centres (glenohumeral under the acromion by de Leva's share, the elbow, stylion), `u` anterior, `v` lateral. The arm's tapes are taken square to the arm's axis (ANSUR II), so the square sections are their sites. |
| Stations and tapes | deltoid's greatest breadth, biceps (`upperArmCircumference` midway acromion to olecranon), elbow (`elbowCircumference`, epicondyles), forearm's greatest girth (`forearmCircumference`; its site needs a law), wrist (`wristCircumference` at stylion). |
| Front view (lateral and medial outlines) | at the deltoid the lateral outline stands at `bideltoidBreadth / 2` from the midline, measured arms hanging, so in the bone frame it is `bideltoidBreadth / 2` less the glenohumeral centre's lateral place (frame-invariant transfer; #3045 reads the breadth); `elbowBreadth` (bony) and `wristBreadth` (bony) plus soft tissue at their sites (Heinz 2003 gives both breadths on their girths); elsewhere the girth with a width-to-depth law (law needed). |
| Side view | from the girth and the law; the olecranon a knot of the posterior profile at the elbow. |
| Inside | cubital fossa: a diff, a pit along the anterior normal at the elbow crease between brachioradialis and pronator teres (width law needed), depth bounded by `upperArmFront` and `forearm`; olecranon: a merge if its own law is sourced, otherwise the posterior profile's knot. Deltoid heads, biceps and triceps masses lie along the arm (gap 2). |
| Join to the trunk | the arm is the part, the trunk's girdle the host. Seed at the axilla vault (`axillaHeight`), the curve running up the anterior and posterior axillary folds and over the deltoid's origin line; the axilla and the shoulder are its blend, `R` from `upperArmBack` and `chest`. The hand outside the thigh at the neutral (`test_human_arm_clearance`) is a placement check of the composed figure. |
| Replaces | `AutoMovieHumanArmRows`, the row channels of `AutoMovieHumanArmGuides`, `AutoMovieHumanArmGirth`'s girth scaling, `AutoMovieHumanDeltoidSpan`, `AutoMovieHumanUpperArmCompartments`, `AutoMovieHumanElbowSpan`, `AutoMovieHumanForearmBelly`, `AutoMovieHumanWristSpan` (as row pieces; any sourced law in them moves to an outline knot), `AutoMovieHumanArmScye`, the arm's `AutoMovieHumanLimbRootStrip`, `ARM_ARC`, `AutoMovieHumanArmRootArc`, `AutoMovieHumanArmRootOval`, `AutoMovieHumanShoulderCap`, `AutoMovieHumanArmPrior` where it holds rows. |

### Hand (#3051)

| Item | Design |
| --- | --- |
| Palm | the arm walk's last segment: the forearm's outlines run on from stylion into the palm's (the wrist is a station, rule 6), `u` dorsal, `v` radial. The palm's knots: the wrist crease, the knuckle line (`handBreadth` across metacarpale II to V, `handCircumference` there), the hand's thickness at metacarpal III (Greiner 1991, `greiner-1991-hand.md`), the metacarpal arch as the palmar quarters' departure (law needed). The palm ends in a distal cap past the knuckles; `palmLength` places the middle finger's web. |
| Digits | each finger and the thumb one walk from its metacarpophalangeal centre (the thumb's from its carpometacarpal centre) to its tip pole, interphalangeal joints as turns; breadth and dorsal and palmar profiles from the existing digit laws (`AutoMovieHumanDigitSpan`, `buryanov-2010-hand-segments.md`, `reddy-2024-metacarpal-ct.md`, each re-read at the build), `thumbBreadth` at its site. The neutral posture's joint angles replace `hand/AutoMovieHumanHandPosture` (fitted on the references) by a published resting posture (law needed). |
| Joins | each finger merged onto the palm's distal cap, the thumb onto the palm's radial side; the webs are the blends, `R` the smaller digit's half breadth, the saddles closed as corners (gap 3). Thenar and hypothenar: the palm's palmar-radial departure and ulnar outline until gap 2 lets them be merged masses. Creases and nail folds lie along their host (gap 2); nails stay today's plates. |
| Measures | hand breadth and circumference read on the palm's own section at the knuckle line, the palm being a lofted part: `AutoMovieHumanHandSkinTargets` and `AutoMovieHumanKnuckleSection` (the mesh read twice) go. |
| Replaces | `AutoMovieHumanHandEvents`, `AutoMovieHumanHandWeb`, `AutoMovieHumanHandWebCrest`, `AutoMovieHumanBodyWeb`, `generation/web/*`, `AutoMovieHumanHandSkinTargets`, `AutoMovieHumanKnuckleSection`, `AutoMovieHumanHandHeel` (the walk carries the wrist), `AutoMovieHumanPalmRelief`'s ray pads, `AutoMovieHumanDigitJointRelief` and `AutoMovieHumanDigitCrease` (ray reliefs), `hand/AutoMovieHumanHandPosture`. |

### Operator gaps found by this map (to the supervisor for the kernel worker; each blocks the rows named)

1. Merge onto a host that is already a merge. The gluteal fold (thigh against the gluteal mass), the gluteal cleft and the cleavage (a mass against its pair), the malleoli (on the shank-foot blend) each need a curve that crosses from one face of the host (the base, a blend, a merged part) to the next. `AutoMovieHumanSolidJoin` takes one `IAutoMovieHumanSolidSurface` as the host. Needed: a host made of faces with trim loops, the trace switching face where its host point leaves a face's trimmed domain.
2. A part that lies along its host. The trace parametrizes the curve by the part's phase and needs each phase's station line to cross the host once; a groove or a mass lying along its host (the spinal groove, the gastrocnemius heads, vastus medialis, the deltoid heads, the digit creases, nail folds) has station lines that run along the host and cross it twice or never. Needed: the curve traced by another parameter (the angle about the footprint's centre in the host's tangent plane, or arclength), with the rails and blend unchanged. Until then those features stay out or on outline knots, never a carve along rays.
3. Corners where two blends' host rails would cross (the crotch's perineum, the webs, the cleavage, the cleft): the setback closed by `AutoMovieHumanSeamPatch`, which the merge-and-diff design (item 7) left for after the two-part operators.
4. A part trimmed at both ends: the leg is merged onto the trunk at its top and onto the foot at its bottom, so its rails stand at both ends of one chain.
5. Tessellation of the results: the host's trim loop, the part's per-phase start, the blend's columns and the corners into the skin with their loft coordinates (the `IAppendage` path of `AutoMovieHumanBodyRoots` and `AutoMovieHumanBodyStrip` is unexercised); which worker owns it.

Open questions settled by measurement before the build, never by fitting: whether the thighs' medial outlines cross the median plane under the crotch on the population ends at the neutral stance (if they do, the two thighs are a third contact at the perineum, gap 3; the stance is not moved to avoid it); whether the breasts touch at the midline on the full-bust end; the knee's and the elbow's neutral angles; whether one conic per quarter carries the trunk's front and back.

Laws needed from the input and laws worker, each with a published source or a derivation: width-to-depth at the trunk's untaped levels and at every limb tape site; the bone's place inside each limb section; the calf's and the forearm's greatest-girth sites; the underbust depth; the trunk's front and back quarter departures; the trapezius line; soft tissue over the acromion, the epicondyles, the malleoli and stylion; the breast's footprint and pole ratio; the gluteal mass's lateral edge; the navel's size; the patella's size; the palm's metacarpal arch; the neutral hand posture.

| Phenomenon | Cause | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- |
| the masses run into their host, the gluteal ones up through the trunk to the face | `AutoMovieHumanMassPart` set `v = n x u`; the straight axis advances along `v x u = -n`, so every mass's stations ran inward from a start already inside | `v = u x n`, so stations run out along `n` (the mass test's pole over the plane already expected this) |
| the gluteal mass's start sank hundreds of millimetres | the embed's sagitta `kappa e^2 / 2` grows without bound where a footprint edge reaches past the host's turn (the lower edge to the crotch, where the base closes on the perineum) | the circle of curvature's sagitta `R - sqrt(R^2 - e^2)`, an edge past `R` dropping `R` |
| left gluteal's lateral and medial edges swapped (the right breast's too, after the mirror's swap) | `+v` turns side with the normal (forward on a breast, back on a gluteal mass), so no fixed swap per side is right | the part takes the caller's lateral direction and maps lateral and medial by its own frame; the trunk's mirror reflects target and up only |
